宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
假期扰动 PMI走势，内外需双双放缓

[Table_ReportType] 
——10月PMI数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月02日

[Table_Summary] ⚫ 事件： 10月中采制造业PMI为 49.3%，较上月下跌0.8个
百分点，非制造业PMI由50.2%下跌至49.7%。 
⚫ 制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气跌幅较大。PMI
再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响。中、小
型企业PMI回落幅度较大，均处于临近值下方，大型企业
PMI小幅回落，仍处于景气区间。 
⚫ 产需双双回落，进出口景气度降温，价格指标景气度回落。
生产指数降至临界值下方，新订单指数下跌，生产回落快于
需求。新出口订单、进口指数下跌，而欧美日等制造业PMI
景气度整体保持旺盛。企业采购意愿下滑，生产放缓影响企
业采购需求。纺织、有色金属、非金属矿等行业生产、新订
单偏弱。原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企
业成本压力边际缓解，有色金属板块价格跌幅较大，能耗产
业链价格维持高景气。原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的
趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。 
⚫ 高技术、装备制造高位放缓，消费品、基础原材料、钢铁行
业景气度下滑。新经济景气度高位回落，但仍处景气区间，
EPMI大幅拉升，供需持续好转，秋季旺季特征明显，采购
价格持续上行，但速度放缓。SMI中枢上行，中上游制造业
显著修复。消费品行业PMI重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉动
消退后，内需接续效应不足。兰格钢铁PMI下滑幅度较大，
销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，供需均转弱。 
⚫ 建筑业为非制造业PMI主要拖累，服务业PMI处于临界点。
非制造业PMI新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，需求
持续偏弱，价格指标回落。其中，建筑业PMI下滑至临界值
下方，工作日减少带来一定影响，新订单指数跌幅较大，建
筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一
定拖累。服务业PMI小幅放缓，新订单指数跌幅较大，价格
指数回落，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。 
⚫ 季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支
撑。10月受长假和外部扰动等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需
均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售前瞻
指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定
压力，融资环境指数小幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有
望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资好转。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及
预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积 
财政支出有望继续提速

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 3 页 共 7 页  
事件 
2026年10月，中采制造业PMI为49.3%， 较上月下跌0.8个百分点， 非制造业PMI为49.7%，
较上月下跌0.5个百分点，综合PMI为49.7%，环比下跌1个百分点。

1    PMI环比季节性回落，产需双双放缓 
制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气回落幅度较大。制造业PMI环比回落0.8个
百分点至49.3%，再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响，财政投放提速，
项目建设给PMI表现形成支撑。从不同规模企业来看，小型企业PMI下行1.4个百分点
至47.5%， 中型企业PMI下行1.3个百分点至48.4%， 大型企业PMI由50.6%回落至50.2%，
仅大型企业处于景气区间，中小企业依然承压。 
高技术、装备制造高位继续回落，战略新兴产业显著回暖，消费品、基础原材料、钢
铁行业景气度下滑。高技术制造业继续下行1.2个百分点至51.3%，装备制造业由51%
回落为50.6%，高端制造业景气度持续回落，但仍处景气区间。基础原材料行业由48%
回落为47.3%。消费品行业下行1.9个百分点至48.8%，重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉
动消退后，内需接续效应不足。战略性新兴产业PMI（EPMI）由52.3%拉升至58%，达
到年内高点，其中，生产量、产品订货和进口指数上行幅度较大，供需双双好转，秋
季旺季特征明显， 但进口指数仍处荣枯线下方， 采购价格持续上行， 但速度明显放缓，
就业和研发指标也有所上行。光伏经理人指数（SMI）10月中枢较9月上行，中上游制
造业显著修复，下游电站小幅回落。兰格钢铁PMI由49.8%回落为46.9%，下滑幅度较
大，其中，销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，下行至荣枯线下方，销量、总订单、国内
订单也明显回落，出口订单量小幅下滑，仅库存水平环比上行。

图表1：10月制造业PMI环比季节性回落（%） 图表2：高技术制造、装备制造业PMI继续回落（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

口指数环比转为下跌2.2个百分点至47%。采购量下行2.1个百分点至48.9%，生产放缓
影响企业采购需求回落。供货商配送时间由50.1%上行至50.2%。从统计局公布数据来
看，铁路船舶航空航天设备、计算机通信电子设备等行业生产指数和新订单指数均位
于53.0%及以上；纺织、非金属矿物制品、有色金属冶炼及压延加工等行业生产指数
和新订单指数均低于临界点，地产持续偏弱对上游的拖累作用持续，部分有色金属价
格、库存偏高给产需带来一定影响。美国制造业PMI高位边际放缓，但景气度仍强，
日本制造业PMI持续小幅回落，仍处荣枯线上方。德国、英国制造业PMI上行，处于临
界点上方，法国受国内动乱影响，制造业PMI降至荣枯线下方。 
图表3：供需分项环比回落，价格指标下行（%） 图表4：美国制造业PMI小幅回落，景气仍强（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 
价格指标景气度回落，原材料和加工行业剪刀差收窄，有色行业价格下滑为主要拖
累，能化产业链维持高景气。主要原材料购进价格回落4个百分点至56.8%，出厂价格
下行2.3个百分点至51.7%，原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企业成本
压力边际缓解。10月，受到宏观流动性收紧预期和基本面供需矛盾等因素影响，有色
金属板块承压，金、银、钴、碳酸锂等有色金属跌幅较大。国际油价持续攀升，石油
煤炭及其他燃料加工、化学纤维及橡胶塑料制品等行业价格指数维持高景气。 
原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。产成品库
存由47.6%下跌至47.5%， 原材料库存上行0.2个百分点至48.4%。原料端价格压力持续，
企业备库需求和价格增长带动原材料库存上行。生产经营活动预期指标由53.8%回落
为53.4%。 
图表5：原材料库存累积，产成品库存去化（%） 图表6：原材料价格和出厂价格价差明显收窄（%）

2    非制造业PMI回落，建筑业为主要拖累 
非制造业PMI回落，需求持续偏弱，价格指标回落。10月非制造业PMI为49.7%，较上
月下跌0.5个百分点。其中，新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，持续处于临近
值下方，在手订单指数也有所回落。商务活动指数下滑，降至临近值下方。投入品价
格回落幅度大于销售价格回落幅度。业务活动预期由55.5%回落至54.7%，企业经营预
期下跌，但仍处景气区间。仅从业人员由45.9%小幅上行至46%。 
建筑业PMI下滑至临界值下方，新订单指数下滑幅度较大，工作日减少带来一定影响，
销售价格和投入品价格走势分化。建筑业PMI下跌至48%，上月为50.3%。其中，建筑
业新订单指数下跌5.7个百分点至40%， 建筑业业务活动预期下跌3.1个百分点至52.3%，
建筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一定拖累。随着财政资金
落地， “六张网”建设逐步显效，有望带动建筑业回暖。 
服务业PMI处于临界点，新订单指数持续偏弱，价格指数回落。服务业PMI为50%，较
上月下跌0.2个百分点。服务业新订单指数下跌，回落幅度相对最大。服务业投入品
和销售价格均下跌，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。业务活动预期指数小
幅回落，仍处临界值上方。从统计局公布数据来看，电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、
货币金融服务、保险等行业商务活动指数持续位于55.0%以上的高景气区间；资本市
场服务、房地产等行业商务活动指数低位运行。

图表7：建筑业PMI新订单跌幅较大（%） 图表8：服务业新订单下跌(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

制造业用工需求持平，服务业就业好转，建筑业就业回落。10月制造业PMI从业人员
持平于上月的48.4%。服务业从业人员较上月上行0.5个百分点至46.7%，建筑业从业
人员指数为42%，较上月下跌2.3个百分点，十一长假影响建筑业用工需求，给服务业
就业带来提振。

3    总结与展望 
季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支撑。10月受长假和外部扰动
等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售
前瞻指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定压力，融资环境指数小
幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资
好转。

图表9：企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落（%） 图表10：企业融资环境指数小幅上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

4    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。

[Table_RiskInfo] 风险提示 
我们在此提醒您，不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的
相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考
虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。

出具本报告。本报告清晰准确地反映了本人的研究观点。本人不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意

公司簡介 
東陽成立於 1967 年，主營項目為汽、機車
零組件 ， 為台灣塑膠件供應商 ， 也是全球第
一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球
市佔率 70%、 板金部份市佔率38%。2Q26 
AM 營收佔比 75.68%，OEM 為 24.32%。

本次報告：電話訪談

東陽 (1319 TT) 
AM 產業長期正向

永豐觀點 
預估 2027 年 AM 營收增長 5~8%、OEM 年增 1~3%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進建議 ：預估 2026、2027 年營收為 242.9、261 億元(-3.2%、+7.5%YoY)、
毛利率 32.7%、34%，營業利益為 40.5、49 億元(-12%、+21%YoY)，稅後淨利
為 36.1、40.2 億元(-4.9%、+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 6.12、6.79 元。看好東陽最差
情況已過，即將迎來旺季，出貨將逐步重返正常增長軌道。目前評價交易在
13X2026 年 EPS(F)、11.9X2027 年 EPS(F)，為近五年本益比區間(10~18X)下緣，
本次以 2027 年 EPS 進行評價，給予 15X 本益比，目標價 102 元(15X2027 年(F) 
EPS 6.79 元)，維持買進建議。

均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。

公司簡介：東陽成立於 1967 年，主營項目為汽、機車零組件，為台灣塑膠件供應
商，也是全球第一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球市佔率 70%、板金部份
市佔率 38%。2Q26 AM 營收佔比 75.68%，OEM 為 24.32%。AM 營收分布為北
美 71%、中南美 11%、歐洲 11%、中東 3%。OEM 市場為中國 64%、台灣 36%。
1995 年起，公司陸續在中國重慶、南京、福州、長春、哈爾濱、敦陽、長春、天
津、武漢、長沙、廣州等地設廠，中國 OEM(非合併)主要客戶包含一汽 64%、特
斯拉 11%、長安汽車 10%、廣汽 4%。 
關稅底定，北美 AM 客戶回補庫存，3Q26 AM 營收年增 15.7%，重回成長：美
國 5 月下旬將台灣輸美汽車零件的 232 條款關稅調降至 15% 後，客戶結束觀
望，回補先前壓低的庫存，拉貨逐月增加，目前東陽工廠稼動率逾 90%，採兩班
制 24 小時生產。3Q26 AM 營收 45 億元(+5% QoQ、+15.7% YoY)，9 月 AM 營
收 14.96 億元(+0.85% MoM、+7.6% YoY)。 
AM 產業長期正面：北美維修量降幅收斂、副廠件占比創高；北美旺季即將到
來，客戶補庫存可期：北美碰撞維修量已從 2025 年的大幅衰退回到接近持平，
副廠件占比創新高，產業面已落底，結構變化對東陽長期有利： 
1. 汽車維修產業面：維修量衰退已收斂，且AM 件占比創新高：依美國理賠估價
平台 CCC Intelligent Solutions 統計(下簡稱 CCC)，2026 年 1~9 月全美理
賠件數降幅由去年同期的 8.3%縮小至 3.5%，可修復估價件數由 10.4%縮小
至 5.4%。北美大型汽車維修連鎖企業 Boyd 估計 2Q26 可修復理賠量持平至
年減 2%，與 CCC 方向一致。另，依 CCC 統計，2025 年副廠件占全美碰撞
換件金額 22.7%，為歷史新高；在維修量下滑的環境下，副廠件金額仍成長  
1.7%，原廠件則減少 1.8%。綜合上述，產業面已落底，有望回溫，而結構方
面，副廠件使用率持續提升，東陽為受惠者。 
2. 東陽客戶面：主要客戶 LKQ 於北美 AM 碰撞件優於市場：LKQ 2Q26 北美
零件及服務有機營收成長 0.5%，為九季來首度轉正，其中 AM 碰撞件營收
成長約 2%；同時，LKQ 公布其替代零件使用率(APU，即維修時選用副廠件、
而非原廠件的比例)連續兩季創下歷史新高，已突破 40%，背後原因是美國保
險公司近期理賠成本上升壓力較大，加上二手車價格走高，促使保險公司更傾

營運現況與分析 
圖一：東陽主要生產汽車塑膠及鈑金件

資料來源：東陽，Oct. 2026

### Page 3

3

向要求維修廠使用價格較低的副廠零件以控制成本 ， 顯示副廠零件滲透率提升
是長期趨勢，而非短期景氣波動。 
3. 研究部研判：AM 最差情況已過。維修量降幅收斂、副廠件占比提升，加上關
稅底定後的補庫存，再加上東陽每年投入平均約 18 億元開發新模具(今年為
25 億元)、持續擴大北美與歐洲保險公司認證，支撐 AM 營收回到成長軌道、
長期向好。惟 4Q25 受暴風雪帶動、基期較高，若今年冬天偏暖，則 4Q26
年增幅度可能有限；1Q27 則因 1Q26 低基期，年增表現較有利。 
短期負面因子：貨櫃仍偏緊，緩解速度取決於旺季強度：目前待出貨訂單累積 200
多櫃訂單，等待客戶安排貨櫃艙位，緩解速度需視旺季強度(採 FOB 報價，過往客
戶在需求好的時候願意加價取得貨櫃)。研究處認為目前客戶庫存約 1 個月，且旺
季(11 月~隔年 3 月)到來，預期有望逐步緩解。 
4Q26 毛利率有機會優於 3Q26：旺季稼動率有機會達 90%~95%(目前東陽工廠
稼動率逾 90%)，可攤提模具折舊等固定成本(AM 模具折舊約占 AM 成本 17%)。
旺季時北美銷售比重由淡季約 65%~66%回升至約 70%，有助產品組合。而市場
擔憂原物料上升的部分，截至九月來看，PP 較戰前上漲 5%，ABS 為 24%，但因
塑膠原料約 90%使用 PP、ABS 僅約 10%， 且東陽議價能力強 ， 成本上漲可轉嫁，
今年以來東陽產品售價已調整約 1.1%~1.5%，占比近 30%的自有產品轉嫁較快，
其餘產品約需一季~半年轉嫁，綜合以上，原物料上漲的部分對整體毛利率影響不
大。主要影響毛利率的因素在於產品組合(北美營收佔比、塑膠件比重)，以及稼動
率，故預期 4Q26、1Q27AM 毛利率將有望回升至 40%以上。 
預估 2027 年東陽 AM 營收重回正常成長軌道，年增 5~8%；OEM 年增 1~3%：
東陽是全球第一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球市佔率 70%、板金部份市
佔率 38%，其品項覆蓋範圍最廣，且模具持續增加，故在市況不明朗時，東陽仍
佔據產業領先地位及強議價能力，東陽2026 年預估將有 500~600 項 AM 新品通
過認證，新產品毛利率較高，搭配 Q4~Q1 迎來傳統旺季，預估 AM 營收有望持
續回溫。AM 市場的競爭重點便是模具數及規模經濟，參考東陽於 2024-2025 年
擴大模具開發套數及擴充產能，將有助於未來幾年 AM 營收的增長。而 OEM 方
面，台灣車市有望逐步復甦，且東陽客戶新案陸續放量，有望支撐2027 年營運表
現。中國 OEM 方面，今明年重點在於電動車轉型持續發酵，客戶展望正面，雖然
前幾年陸續關廠導致營收規模持續縮減， 但近期已調整告一段落， 預期整體產能利
用率能夠提升，推升毛利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估2027 年東陽 AM 營收重
回正常成長軌道，年增 5~8%；OEM 年增 1~3%。 
維持買進建議，長線投資價值顯現：預估 2026、2027 年營收為 242.9、261 億元
(-3.2%、+7.5%YoY)、毛利率 32.7%、34%，營業利益為 40.5、49 億元(-12%、
+21%YoY)，稅後淨利為 36.1、40.2 億元(-4.9%、+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 6.12、

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ 
～物価上振れリスクが強まれば、一段の利上げ加速も～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
池田 亮平 
エコノミスト 阿部 大樹 
シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山 祐資 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 日銀は物価上振れ抑制に舵。中東情勢に起因する物価押し上げリスクは予断を許さず 
9 月日銀会合後の会見で植田総裁は「政策局面が変化した」と述べた（図表 1） 。これまで日銀は、
基調的物価の押し上げを金融政策運営の重点としてきたが、足元では 2％前後の物価上昇が定着しつ
つあるとみられる。こうしたもとで、金融政策運営の重点は物価上振れの回避に移っており、そのた
めに緩和的な金融環境をなるべく早く脱することが重視される。日銀の基本的なスタンスが、連続利
上げや、0.25％の利上げ幅を上回る大幅利上げは回避するのが望ましいというものであることも踏ま
えると、今後の利上げペースは 3 カ月に一回程度、0.25％ずつが基本になるだろう 1。 
ただし、金融政策の重点が物価上振れ回避にシフトしているなかでは、よりフォワードルッキング
な政策運営が求められる 2。したがって、 原油調達コストの上昇や円安など、 物価上振れリスクの強ま
り次第で利上げペースが速まる可能性がある。先行きの物価を押し上げる主なリスク要因として、日
図表1 9月会合後の総裁会見のポイント 図表2 WTI原油先物

### Page 2

2

銀は、原油高等の中東情勢影響、AI 関連需要の強さ、円安の三点を挙げている。このうち、原油価格
は足元で再び上昇しており、先行きの不確実性も大きい。中東情勢は、物価を取り巻く現下最大のリ
スクだろう。 
■ 不安定な情勢下で再び原油価格が上昇。タンカー運賃も急騰しダブルパンチの様相 
WTI原油先物は、9月に入って一時1バレル=100ドル台と、5月中旬以来の水準に上昇した （図表2） 。
足元では原油価格は幾分低下しているものの、水準としては依然高い状態が続いている。背景には、
中東産原油の供給懸念が強まったことがある。7月下旬以降、 サウジアラビアとイエメン・フーシ派に
よる戦闘が開始し、 ホルムズ海峡を代替する重要な原油輸送ルートとして活用されてきたバブ ・ エル ・
マンデブ海峡の通航リスクが高まった。 また、9月に入り、 サウジアラビアの原油パイプラインが攻撃
を受けて停止したと伝わると、中東産原油の域外輸送が一段と困難になるとの見方が広がった。 
中東情勢が再度悪化するなかで上昇しているのは原油価格だけではない。原油価格の上昇を大幅に
上回る勢いで、原油タンカーの運賃が急騰している（図表3）。8月以降に上昇基調が鮮明となり、9月
中旬には一時、中東情勢悪化前（2026年初）の約46倍、情勢悪化後の3～7月頃と比べても約3.2倍に達
した。 海峡航行リスクが高まるもと、 積み替えや迂回によって原油輸送の効率が悪化しているためだ。
海事データを活用して商品市場を分析しているベルギーの調査会社Kplerによると、 ホルムズ海峡の内
側にあるペルシャ湾から原油を運び出す船と、海峡の外側から最終目的地まで運ぶ船を分け、洋上で
原油を積み替える方式が広がっているようだ。 長距離輸送を担う船が危険な航路を通らずに済む反面、
積み替え作業や待機に時間を要するため、船を次の輸送に回しにくくなる。また、危険な海域を避け
るために遠回りする輸送も増え、船が一度の航海に費やす日数も長くなっている。より多くの輸送資
源が必要となったことで船舶需給がひっ迫し、タンカー運賃が大きく押し上げられている格好だ。

図表3 原油タンカーの運賃 図表4 原油輸入単価と原油価格

当たり換算収益。日本を含む東アジア向け運賃の代理指標。

### Page 3

3

■ 今後は日本の原油調達コストが上昇。企業間取引価格は反転加速を予想 
原油価格の上昇に原油タンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの日本の原油輸入単価（単位量あたり
の原油調達コスト）は一段と上昇する見込みだ3。 中東産原油が日本に輸送されるまでには1～2カ月程
度の期間を要するため、8月中旬以降の原油価格上昇 ・原油タンカー運賃上昇は10月以降の原油輸入単
価を押し上げ、その後国内の企業間取引価格へと影響が波及していくと考えられる。足元の原油価格
とタンカー運賃を踏まえると、10月の原油輸入単価は1バレル＝約140ドル程度と、中東情勢悪化後で
最も高い水準に上昇する可能性が高い（図表4）。この影響を輸入全体の平均価格（円建てベース）で
みると、10月の伸びは前年比＋29％程度と、中東情勢悪化後に最も輸入物価の伸びが加速した6・7月
に迫る勢いになる計算だ。さらに、その後は、石油化学製品などの関連財を中心に、国内の企業間取
引価格が上昇しよう。 足元で上昇圧力が一服しつつある国内企業物価は再び加速し、11月頃に同＋7％
台の伸びになると予想する（図表5）。 
加えて、原油輸入金額が膨らんで貿易収支が悪化すれば、円安圧力が強まる可能性がある。原油の
代替調達が進展した6月以降は1兆円に迫る貿易赤字が続いているが、特に10月以降は原油輸入単価が
上昇するもとで本邦貿易赤字が拡大する公算が大きく、実需面からの円安圧力が強まりやすい（図表
6） 。 足元の為替相場は、 米国の利上げ観測を背景に円安に振れやすい地合いだ。 そこに実需の動きが
重なり、1ドル＝160円を上回るようなドル高円安が進行すれば、輸入物価の上振れを通じて、国内企
業物価の伸びが一層高まるリスクがある。 
■ 物価上昇圧力が長期化する場合は利上げペース加速も視野に 
以上のように、当面の企業間取引価格に対しては押し上げ圧力が持続する公算が大きい。物価上昇
圧力が長期化するほど消費者物価への価格転嫁姿勢も強まる可能性があるだけに、日銀は物価上振れ
に対する高い警戒レベルを維持することになろう。 
仮に物価上振れリスクが大きく高まる場合には、 連続利上げ ・大幅利上げの可能性も排除できない。
中東情勢の混乱が長期化し、27年以降も原油輸入単価が一段と上振れることが見込まれる場合、ある

### Page 4

4

いは1ドル=160円を持続的に上回る水準まで円安が進展する場合には 、連続利上げや大幅利上げのシ
ナリオが現実味を帯びてくるだろう。

1 中立金利の水準は不透明であり、利上げの影響はタイムラグをともなって現れる。連続利上げ・大幅利上げによって意図せず
金融引き締めに転じれば、経済へのマイナス影響が必要以上に強まるリスクがある。 
2 中東情勢悪化以降で最初の利上げを決定した 6 月会合を振り返っても、日銀は川上の企業物価の加速を理由の一つとして利上
げに踏み切っている。6 月会合の声明文において日銀は、国内企業物価上昇率が前年比＋6.3％（5 月時点の速報ベース）に加速
したことを踏まえ、 「企業間取引におけるやや速いスピードでの価格転嫁が、消費者段階における幅広い品目の価格上昇に波及
していく可能性がある」と評価した。 
3 中東情勢悪化以降、日本の原油輸入単価は国際市況から大きく上振れている。背景として、主な代替調達先である米国産原油
が需給ひっ迫の懸念等から値上がりし、割高になったことなどが挙げられる。経済産業省発表によると、秋口以降は中東産原油
の調達が増加し、割高な米国産原油に対する依存度は低下すると見込まれるものの、本文記載のように中東産原油の輸送コスト
が大幅に高まっていることから、先行きも割高感の解消は見込みづらい。

上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。人民幣中間價連續走升，接連升破 6.7700、6.7600 整數
關口，升至三年七個月來高點，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美元指數震盪走強，加
以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，
週四聯準會鷹派升息一碼，惟人民幣中間價連續五日走升至 6.7521，並創下四年高
點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪走升 。 就國際市場來看 ，FED 最新政策聲明和經濟預測顯
示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6700-6.7200

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.80-159.50 
前週交易區間 
153.29 158.05

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，聯準會升息預期升溫加上歐元區 9 月經濟景氣指
數下滑，令歐元走跌至 1.1525；週間，美國聯準會鷹派升息立場帶動美元走升，歐
元最低來到 1.1460；臨近週末，歐元持續偏弱整理，終場收在 1.1483，週線下跌 107
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、 歐洲央行管委卡薩克斯
(Martins Kazaks) 表示 ， 為避免美伊戰爭引發的燃料成本上漲開始傳導至工資和其他
商品價格，歐洲央行可能需要漸進式調升利率以抑制通膨。最新利率期貨市場顯示
歐洲央行今年仍有升息一碼的空間，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國聯準會決議將
利率調升一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，聯準會主席華許會後偏鷹言論帶動美元指數走
升至 100 上方，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自
前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解、能源
價格持續高位情況下，市場對於歐元區經濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度，將不利歐元
表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

2026年 10月 30日 全7頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月鉱工業生産 
自動車工業が挽回生産で持ち直し、生産全体は3カ月ぶりに上昇

経済調査部 エコノミスト ビリング 安奈

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調
査）を上回り、3 カ月ぶりに上昇した。自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直したほか、AI
関連需要で生産用機械工業なども増産し、生産全体を押し上げた。経済産業省は基調
判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
◼ 先行きの生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内生産を
押し上げよう。ただし、金利高による海外需要の下振れなどが生産を下押しするリス
クには注意が必要だ。 
◼ 2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行 CIが前月差▲0.9ptの
117.1、一致CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する。この予測値に基づくと、9月の基調
判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

【生産】自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直し、AI関連需要で生産用機械工業も増産 
2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調査）を
上回り、3カ月ぶりに上昇した。経済産業省は基調判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
生産指数を業種別に見ると、15業種中 8業種が前月から上昇した。自動車工業（前月比＋
5.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。品目別では、普通乗用車（同＋3.3％）や駆動伝導・操縦装
置部品（同＋7.0％）などが増産となった。前月は熊本地震や台風による工場稼働停止や部品
供給の停滞を受けて生産が落ち込んだが、9 月は生産体制がおおむね正常化したことで挽回生
産が進み、自動車工業の生産は持ち直した。また、生産用機械工業（同＋3.4％）は4カ月連続
で上昇した。品目別では、AI関連製品である半導体製造装置（同＋13.8％）のほか、化学機械
（同＋34.9％）などが増産となった。経済産業省「鉱工業出荷内訳表」によると、生産用機械
工業は国内向け（同＋7.8％）が好調だった。汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）では、コン
ベヤ（同＋142.0％）や運搬用クレーン（同＋35.5％）が増産となり、2カ月ぶりの上昇となっ
た。一方、鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業（前月比▲1.6％）や窯業・土石製品工業（同▲1.9％）など 7
業種は前月から低下した。鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業では亜鉛めっき鋼板（同▲10.0％）などが、窯
業・土石製品工業ではガラス繊維（短繊維）（同▲6.3％）などが減産となった。 
財別では、生産財（前月比＋1.0％）、耐久消費財（同＋5.3％）、非耐久消費財（同＋0.9％）、
建設財（同＋2.5％）、資本財（除．輸送機械） （同＋1.0％）のいずれも増産となった。設備投
資の関連指標である資本財（除．輸送機械）はAI需要を背景に大幅な増産が見込まれる。

【出荷・在庫】出荷指数は自動車工業などを中心に2カ月ぶりに上昇 
2026年 9月の出荷指数は前月比＋0.6％と 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。業種別では、自動車工業
（同＋2.3％）や汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋5.5％）など 15業種中 8業種が上昇した。財別に
見ると、非耐久消費財と資本財（除．輸送機械）、建設財は低下した一方、耐久消費財と生産
財は上昇した。在庫指数は同＋2.5％、在庫率指数は同＋2.0％だった。

生産予測調査による。

【先行き】生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み 
先行きの生産指数は、緩やかな上昇基調を辿るとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内
生産の押し上げ要因となる。ただし、世界的な金利高、特に米国の金利高による海外需要の下
振れなどが生産を下押しするリスクには注意が必要だ。 
AI関連財の拡大は 2024年から日本国内の生産を押し上げてきており、今後も生産の押し上
げ要因になると見込まれる1。26年 8月の世界半導体販売額（3カ月平均）を見ると、前年比＋
144.3％と強い伸びが続いている。米国の販売額（同＋180.9％）がけん引する中、日本（同＋
62.3％）2も力強さを示している。また、日本半導体製造装置協会によると、26年9月の半導体
製造装置販売高（3カ月平均）は同＋48.2％と高い伸びとなった3。AI関連を中心とする旺盛な
半導体需要が反映されているとみられる。ただし、米国の金利高でAI関連需要が下振れする可
能性には注意が必要だ。高金利下で米企業の資金調達負担が増え、多額の資金を必要とする AI
関連投資が鈍化すれば4、日本の生産も輸出減を通して下押しされるリスクがある。 
なお、中東情勢による悪影響は引き続き注視する必要がある。米国の中間選挙後に対イラン
攻撃が再開される可能性も報じられており5、原油供給をめぐる不安は引き続き残る。一方、今
年 2月末に始まった中東危機を受け、ホルムズ海峡の輸送停滞への対応として政府が国家備蓄
原油を放出して以降、初めて買い戻しが実施された6。代替調達が進み、国家備蓄原油の取り崩
しが一服し、備蓄の補充が始まったことは好材料だ。ただし、情勢が再び悪化すれば、原材料
不足を通じて生産が下押しされる可能性がある。 
製造工業生産予測調査では、10月の生産指数は前月比＋5.1％と見込まれる。業種別では 11
業種中 9業種が上昇する見通しだ。電子部品・デバイス工業（同＋16.6％）で集積回路などの
増産が計画されているほか、生産用機械工業（同＋15.5％）では半導体・フラットパネルディ
スプレイ製造装置など、AI関連製品の増産が全体を押し上げる見通しだ。生産指数全体の計画
のバイアスを補正した試算値（最頻値）7で見ると、10月は同＋3.9％と上昇が見込まれる。 
11月の生産指数は前月比＋0.8％と見込まれている。業種別では 11業種中 7業種が上昇する
見通しだ。輸送機械工業（同＋4.3％）では乗用車など、汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）
では計測・分析機器などの増産が見込まれている。

1 詳細は、神田慶司・秋元虹輝・菊池慈陽・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年6月」（大和総研レポート、
2026年6月23日）を参照。

【26年9月景気動向指数】先行CIは低下、一致CIは上昇を見込む 
鉱工業指数の結果を受けて、2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行
CIが前月差▲0.9ptの 117.1、一致 CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する（図表 4） 。先行CIで
は構成指標のうち、鉱工業用生産財在庫率指数（逆サイクル）や最終需要財在庫率指数（逆サ
イクル）、東証株価指数などが前月から悪化した。一致 CIでは構成指標のうち、生産指数（鉱
工業）や鉱工業用生産財出荷指数、有効求人倍率（除学卒）などが改善した。この予測値に基
づくと、2026年9月の基調判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

図表 4：景気動向指数（先行 CI、一致 CI）と基調判断の推移

Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

ab 27 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Faraday Technology
New opportunities in advanced nodes
Q326 beat on turnkey strength and non-op gains; Q426 outlook positive
Q326 EPS of NT$1.07 came in above UBS/Street expectations mainly on  non-op gains.
Q326 sales came in at NT$3.66 bn (+11% QoQ), ahead of its original guidance but in
line with its comment  during Taiwan Summit with turnkey sales up 20% QoQ given
better support from OSAT and foundries, despite NRE declining 31% QoQ. Q326 GM

decreased 2.7ppt QoQ to 42.5% on higher turnkey mix, while  OPEX grew 9% QoQ  on
salary adjustment and higher bonus accrual. Non-op gain of NT$266mn mainly includes
valuation gains on an investee company. For Q426F, it guided sales  flat  QoQ remaining
at high levels with IP and NRE up QoQ, and turnkey down QoQ. GM is guided back to
~45%, reflecting normalized ASIC economics; OPEX should decline single digit QoQ.
2027 growth outlook supported by AI, advanced packaging and 12nm node

Management guided for double-digit sales growth in 2027, with all three businesses, IP ,
NRE and turnkey, expected to grow, driven by three key catalysts: AI, advanced
packaging and new UMC 12nm project contribution. For IP , growth should be
supported by higher royalties on foundry price hikes/customer capacity expansion and
initial  contribution from 12nm node; NRE should benefit from 2nm/4nm projects
entering peak recognition, with 3-4 ASIC tape-outs targeted in H127; while MP should

see growth in both MCU and ASIC as new projects gradually ramp. AI-related mix was at
8-9% of Q326 sales, and is expected to increase throughout 2027 alongside
accelerating advanced-packaging shipments. We expect 2027E sales to grow 37% YoY.
2nm design win opens new advanced-node and product opportunities
Faraday secured a 2nm GAA special purpose  ASIC project worth several tens of millions
of US dollars in NRE, which management described as its largest-ever design win and

most advanced project to date. The project spans front-end design and IP integration,
back-end design, customized 3D DRAM and advanced packaging, and marks Faraday's
first customized 3D DRAM and hybrid-bonding project. It expects to recognize its first
NRE milestone  in H127, followed by MP targeted within 1-2 years. Beyond 2nm, Faraday
is expanding into new applications including OCS, CXL and Si-cap, leveraging its
capabilities in advanced packaging, interposer design and ASIC integration. We believe

successful execution by Faraday and its foundry partner could strengthen Faraday's
positioning in advanced-node projects and create follow-on design-win opportunities.
Valuation: Raise PT to NT$200, stay Neutral
We raise    2026E EPS (reported) from NT$3.43 to NT$3.68 on better Q326, but lower
2027/28E EPS by 4%/1% to factor in softer CE demand and higher OPEX. We raise our
PT to NT$200 from NT$190 using 24x 2027E PE (vs prior 22x). We raise our multiple as

the recent 2nm project win should boost investor confidence, but stay Neutral as the
advanced nodes (4nm/2nm) turnkey revenue recognition depends on the foundry
partner's execution. We await clearer evidence before revisiting our assumptions.

Company Description
Faraday was founded in 1993, spun off from UMC's IP and semiconductor design service
department as the first IC design service company in Asia. It is an IC design and production
service provider that initially focused on specialty applications on mature nodes by partnering

with UMC and gradually upgraded to advanced nodes through working with Samsung. The
company had its IPO on the Taiwan Securities Exchange in 1999.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Faraday using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced nodes migration, further delay of
advanced packaging ramp, and growing competition in specialty projects from China IC
design service companies.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

### Page 1

M
Update
Nuvoton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Losses Widen; BMC Growth and
Japan Restructuring Remain Key
3Q losses widened Q/Q amid lower gross profit, increased opex
and reduced non-operating income. Management sees scope for
faster BMC growth in 2027, with execution and Japan
restructuring critical to delivering earnings growth.
3Q losses widened: Revenue was NT$7.86bn, -1% Q/Q, +8% Y/Y, 3% above our
estimate; gross profit was NT$2.9bn, -3% Q/Q, +13% Y/Y, 6% above our forecast;

gross margin was 37.4%, -0.6ppt Q/Q, +1.7ppt Y/Y, 1.2ppt above our estimate. Loss
per share was NT$1.24, wider Q/Q, Y/Y, and vs. our expectation. Management
attributed gross margin weakness to product mix; back-end supply constraints and
substantial AI-related R&D left operational progress around 1Q behind plan.
Demand remains mixed: Auto demand has stabilized after prior Y/Y declines, but
management sees no clear rebound. PC demand was significantly weaker in 2H26

than in 1H26 with higher content per PC vs. 2025 helping cushion the impact of
lower volumes. Memory cost increases have been passed through on memory-
bundled MPUs, although some customers began pulling back in 4Q26. MCU
repricing reflects foundry and OSAT costs. Most 2027 capacity has been secured but
cost pass-through and supply availability do not establish margin expansion.
BMC and Japan remain the key milestones: Management sees scope for 2027 BMC

Y/Y growth to exceed 2026's, with existing customers and multiple new customers,
including a CSP, contributing. New customer revenue should initially be small with
fuller contributions over one to two years. Current-gen products should dominate
2027 shipments; next-gen volumes may start modestly in 2H27. Japan remains the
source of losses, making the Tower JV restructuring targeted by management for
end-March 2027 central to recovery.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Ethan Jia

STOCK RATINGS

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

程曦資訊(6898) 
買進, TP 88 (潛在報酬 46.67%)

前一日(10/22)收盤價:73 
承銷價:60 
Target Price:88

程曦資訊深耕客服委外市場 超過 30 年，具備長期公部門合作關
係、高客戶續約率及跨產業客服營運經驗，建立穩定營收基礎與市
場競爭優勢。隨 AI 技術逐步導入既有客服流程，有望降低人力成
本並提升營運效率，加上 AI 客服平台及軟體訂閱服務持續發展，
預期將帶動產品組合與 獲利結構改善。此外，公司取得 ISO/IEC 
42001 人工智慧管理系統認證，有助於提升高規格企業客戶的信
任度，進一步拓展民營企業市場。然而考量 AI 新事業仍處於發展
初期，短期研發費用投入較高，實際獲利貢獻仍需時間顯現，綜合
以上，研究部預估 程曦資訊 2027 年 營 收 為 13.99 億元，
Y+9.24%， 毛利率25.55%，Y+1.15ppts， 營業利益率15.22%，
Y+1.39ppts， 稅後純益1.93 億元 ，Y+16.34%，EPS 為 5.86 元，
參考同業本益比區間約 10~18 倍，給予 15 倍本益比評價，目標
價為 88 元，投資建議為買進。 
 程曦資訊(6898.TT)公司簡介 
程曦資訊(6898.TT)成立於 1993 年 8 月，於 2022 年 5 月登錄興櫃
戰略新板，同年 9 月轉至興櫃一般板，並於 2026 年 10 月 23 日正
式掛牌上櫃交易 。 公司為台灣專業企業流程委外(BPO)及客服營運服
務商，深耕客服市場逾 30 年，以「系統、流程、人力」一站式整合
服務為核心，結合 AI 技術與營運管理經驗，提供企業及政府機關完
整的客服委外解決方案。客戶結構涵蓋政府機關、國營事業、金融
保險、電子商務及國際品牌等，具備大型政府專案執行經驗與長期
客戶合作基礎。營運布 局方面，公司於台北、桃園、台中、台南、
高雄及屏東設有據點，並透過子公司程高資訊、點鑽整合行銷及印
尼程曦，分別拓展中南部客服市場、會員數據行銷及東南亞多語系
客服業務，持續擴大服務範疇與市場規模。

4. 主辦券商：兆豐證券、協辦券商_玉山綜合證券。

上櫃前營運回顧(回溯股本以 3.289 億元計算) 
2024(年)營業收入 11.30 億元，毛利率 24.94%，營業利益率
16.57%，稅後純益 1.83 億元，EPS_5.56 元。 
2025(年)營業收入 11.97 億元，毛利率 24.20%，營業利益率
15.76%，稅後純益 1.72 億元，EPS_5.23 元。

主要業務內容 
1 客服營運收入 ：最核心的收入來源 ， 主係提供客服中心之整合建
置、維運與整體營運管理服務，範疇涵蓋人力招募調度、標準化
培訓、排班、知識庫維護與軟硬體設備租賃等。主要細項服務包
含： 
1.1 電話諮詢服務：提供業務諮詢、訂單查詢、電話轉接、三
方通話及案件後送，合作客戶橫括公部門（如行政院人事
總處、勞動部、1999 專線） 、國營事業 （台灣高鐵、台電、
台水）及跨國企業品牌。 
1.2 客訴處理：處理高難度消費者爭議與客訴案例，協助企業
傾聽顧客需求並優化服務流程。 
1.3 主動外撥與行銷伴銷：運用進線銷售、追加銷售與交叉銷
售經驗 ， 協助客戶將客服中心由成本中心轉型為利潤中心 。 
1.4 全通路服務 ： 整合電話 、Webchat、LINE、FB Messenger、
IG 等多軌管道，提供文字談話、論壇管理與社群服務。 
1.5 技術諮詢客服：提供3C、APP 及電機產品之專業技術問題
諮詢 、 線上故障排除與報修支援 （如OPPO、Realme、Plaud 
AI 等客戶） 。

營運現況 
公部門與企業客服專案帶動成長，研發及人事費用增加壓抑獲利 
程曦資訊 1H26 營收為 6.22 億元，Y+11.28%，主要受惠公部門及
企業客戶客服委外需求持續成長，帶動常態性年約服務與任務型專
案收入增加。其中，常態性年約服務涵蓋移工服務、社會住宅及長
照等政府專案，提供穩定營收基礎；任務型專案則受惠普發現金客
服專案認列，加上企業 AI 客服及數位轉型需求增加，推升整體營運
規模。獲利方面，受惠高毛利任務型專案貢獻增加，以及人力調度

AI 客服座艙提升營運效率，軟體訂閱服務推動獲利結構改善 
展望 2027 年，程曦資訊將持續推動 AI 客服座艙與全通路服務平台
整合，透過 AI IVR 、即時知識查詢、自動化通話摘要及智能質檢等
功能，降低客服人員重複性作業與教育訓練成本。隨相關應用逐步
導入既有客服專案，預期可降低營收成長對人力擴編的依賴，改善
傳統 BPO 業務的人力成本結構；另一方面，公司規劃將客服系統與
AI 應用模組轉化為雲端租賃服務，透過月費訂閱及功能加購增加軟
體收入來源， 公司目標 2027 年 AI 相關新業務營收占比 可望 達
5%~8%。考量初期收入規模仍有限，預期2027 年獲利改善主要來
自既有業務效率提升，隨後續訂閱客戶增加，長期有望進一步帶動
毛利率成長。

AI 治理認證強化競爭優勢，民營企業市場拓展擴大成長空間 
程曦資訊目前營收主要來自公部門， 2025 年公部門營收占比達
76%，未來將在既有公部門業務基礎上，積極拓展金融、能源、交
通及電商等民營企業市場，擴大跨產業客戶服務規模。10M26 公司
取得 ISO/IEC 420 01 人工智慧管理系統認證，結合既有 ISO/IEC 
27001 資訊安全管理能力，可望強化AI 應用在資料保護、風險管理
及法規遵循方面的可信賴度，提高承接大型企業 客服專案的競爭
力。另一方面，公司可透過既有客服營運經驗，進一步提供AI 流程
改造 、 數據分析及顧問服務 ， 增加單一客戶的服務項目與收入貢獻。
綜合以上，研究部預估程曦資訊 2027 年營收為 13.99 億元，
Y+9.24%，毛利率 25.55%，Y+1.15ppts，營業利益率 15.22%，
Y+1.39ppts，稅後純益 1.93 億元，Y+16.34%，EPS 為 5.86 元。

結論與建議 
程曦資訊深耕客服委外市場超過 30 年，具備長期公部門合作關係、
高客戶續約率及跨產業客服營運經驗，建立穩定營收基礎與市場競
爭優勢。隨 AI 技術逐步導入既有客服流程，有望降低人力成本並提
升營運效率，加上 AI 客服平台及軟體訂閱服務持續發展，預期將帶
動產品組合與獲利結構改善。此外，公司取得 ISO/IEC 42001 人工
智慧管理系統認證，有助於提升高規格企業客戶的信任度，進一步

拓展民營企業市場。然而考量 AI 新事業仍處於發展初期，短期研發
費用投入較高，實際獲利貢獻仍需時間顯現，綜合以上，研究部預
估程曦資訊 2027 年營收為 13.99 億元 ，Y+9.24%， 毛利率25.55%，

倍，給予 15 倍本益比評價，目標價為 88 元，投資建議為買進。

기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2 만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3 만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY) 를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다.

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM) 에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27 년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35% 를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

Document 312: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing.

per business client has increased steadily since the spring of 2025

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates. 
Exhibit 8: Shippers’ view of available capacity has fallen 
significantly as of October 1, 2026 
BofA Truckload Capacity Indicator by year (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: The Demand Indicator has been sub-60 for the past two 
months after 4.5 months above 
BofA Truckload Demand Indicator (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted. 
Exhibit 10: Transportation and warehousing payroll growth has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses 
Payroll payments per client by industry and business size (monthly, YoY%, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand. 
Exhibit 11: Ocean shipping/container spending volumes were well above their 2023-2025 monthly average in November 
Logistics and freight average payments by category (monthly, indexed, 100 = monthly 2023-2025 average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
I
N THIS  ISSUE
Macro Strategy—The  Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran: Bessent’s Three-Front War and
the Stakes for
Investors: Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury secretary, but
these days he looks more like America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches

from T
okyo, where he is trying to engineer a stronger Japan yen, to the U.S. Treasury
market, where he is battling to keep the 10-year yield from breaking  decisively above
5%, and to T
ehran, where he has become the point man in the escalating U.S. campaign
to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Ho
w Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A decisive victory on
all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets is next to impossible,

while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best laid plans. That said, however,
the U.S. Secretary of the Treasury has signaled that he is ready to take a very  proactive,
pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less encouraging news stems
from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the Treasury secretary.
Market View—Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”: Behind
ever
y Artificial Intelligence (AI)-generated response is a vast physical network of data

centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems, semiconductor facilities
and critical-mineral supply chains. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power,
water, advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and
intensify pressure
on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend
beyon
d AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

2 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.

For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026ECONEWS

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by a continued global selloff in tech
stocks, leading to safe haven flows into US Treasuries, Japanese Go -
vernment bonds and German bunds. As a result, their yields fell des -
pite energy prices surging to or past their Spring highs. Economic data
generally showed continued resilience. French bonds were under rising

pressure, but Moody's unexpected decision to maintain its France ra -
ting unchanged could, at the margin, help stabilise the spread. The Fed,
ECB and BOJ should be on hold next week while signaling further rate
action is likely.
EUROPEAN UNION
A win for securitization. The Council and the European Parliament have
reached a provisional political agreement on measures to revive and
simplify the EU’s securitization market. The updated framework will

reduce banks’ capital requirements for the safest and most well-struc-
tured transactions, notably for a newly introduced “resilient” category
of securitization. As a key component of the “One Europe, One Market”
initiative and the EU’s Savings and Investment Union agenda, this aims
to channel increased lending to households and businesses. The rele -
vant legal texts must now be finalised.
EUROZONE
Expansion continues. The flash PMIs weakened in October, mainly on

the back of a decline in France. The EZ composite index dipped to 51.9
(-1.2 m/m), dragged down by services (51.7, -1.3 m/m), although ma -
nufacturing also dipped (52.4, -0.5 m/m). Both remain in expansion
territory, and manufacturing new export orders rose to their highest
level since early 2022. Price signals were also rather reassuring, with
the services output price index easing to 54.9 (-1.7 m/m). Households
are more cautious: consumer confidence fell for a second consecutive

month to a five-month low (-17.9). Households’ inflation expectations
edged up at short horizons (+0.1 pp to 3.6% at one year and 3.1% at
three years), but long-term expectations remained anchored, with the
five-year median stable at 2.5%.
France
Rising market stress despite decent economic news; a reprieve from
Moody’s. The spread on French 10-year bond relative to Germany’s
peaked to 174 bps on Friday, the highest since 2011 and only 16 bps
short of its all-time high, as markets price in anticipation of worst-case

scenarios regarding the country’s ability to repair its public finances.
The 10Y yield briefly exceeded 5.1% before falling back to 5% (above
the US equivalent). Nonetheless, services output was stable in August,
following a 0.6% m/m increase in July, a much higher pace than in Q2.
The rebound is driven by the catering sector, the digital sector, business
services, and real estate. The INSEE business climate improved from
96 to 97 (long-term average = 100) in October, but the survey was

conducted before the riots. The main improvement was in manufactu-
ring, which rose from 101 to 105, driven by increased new orders in the
aeronautics sector, and to a lesser extent by the services sector (from
98 to 99). The PMI survey (conducted partially after the riots) was
less positive: the composite index fell to 48.4 (-2.7 m/m), with a sharp
decline in services (48.4; -2.8 m/m) and a slightly smaller decline in
manufacturing (49.3; -1.3 m/m). Moody's unexpectedly announced af -

ter the market close on Friday that it was maintaining France’s ra -
ting at Aa3 on account of France’s diversified economic structure and
sound public institutions, but maintained its negative outlook. This is
on par with the UK and one notch above S&P and Fitch. Moody’s made
clear keeping this rating is conditional on a budget being adopted that
reduces the fiscal deficit, along with an easing of social and market
stress going forward. Saturday’s demonstrations across the country

saw strong attendance but only limited violent incidents.
Germany
Prices continue to rise, but confidence surveys remain on an upward
trend. Producer prices rose 6.7% y/y in September (+2.1pp m/m; the
highest level in 3.5 years) due to higher prices for energy and inter -
mediate goods, primarily metals. The ZEW Economic Sentiment Index
improved beyond consensus forecasts in October and returned to its
highest level since May 2023 (-35.9; +11.2 m/m), albeit the Economic

Expectations Index fell to its lowest level since July (28.1; -6.6 m/m).
The recovery in economic activity continues, although a slowdown is
evident: the flash Composite PMI slowed (52.8; -1 m/m; the lowest in
2 months) due to weaker growth in the services sector (51.7; -1.1 m/m;
the lowest in 2 months) and in manufacturing output (55.1; -0.9 m/m;
the lowest in 3 months). Nevertheless, in both sectors, hiring is rising
slightly, supported by growth in export sales and the spillover effects of

investment plans. Despite persistently high input prices, selling prices
are slowing (reaching their lowest level since February in the manu -
facturing sector).
UNITED KINGDOM
Inflation, wage growth and activity accelerate. CPI inflation overshot
consensus on energy prices, printing at 0.4% m/m and 3.5% y/y in Sep-
tember (consensus: 3.4%, prior: 3.1%), its highest in 11 months. Core
CPI came in at 2.8% y/y (up 0.2 pp), in line with consensus. Pressures

remained persistent according to the PPI at 8.3% y/y (prior: 6.7%). La -
bour market and activity data also surprised positively. Payroll growth
reached 102k in August (3m/3m), bringing the unemployment rate to
4.7% (down 0.2 pp), its lowest since July 2025. October PMI surveys
beat expectations in both manufacturing (52.8, consensus: 51.8, prior:
51.9) and services (53.1, consensus: 51.8, prior: 52.1). The only negative
news came from headline retail sales, falling 0.4% m/m in September,

with the core gauge (excluding petrol) deteriorating by -0.7% m/m.
UNITED STATES
Construction struggling, GENIUS Act implemented. Housing starts
dropped -5.2% m/m in September (-7.4% y/y). This third consecutive
monthly decline dragged the Q3 average to its lowest since Q2 2020.
Building permits also came in negative, at -0.2% m/m (-3.0% y/y), while
the NAHB homebuilder sentiment index plunged to its weakest since
2012. The average 30-year fixed mortgage rate eased to 7.32% (still

134bp above pre-war level), as the ongoing AI selloff pushed down
US treasury yields. The GENIUS Act final implementation rule from the
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency’s (OCC) leaves the door open
for stablecoin customers to receive yield or rewards. Issuers remain
prohibited from paying holders any form of interest or yield solely
in connection with the holding, use, or retention of payment stable -
coins. This represents a shift from the OCC’s February proposal and

has renewed concerns within the banking industry about deposit flight.
The rule will take effect on January 18, 2027.
JAPAN
Activity and inflation keep rising. Composite PMI was unchanged in
September at 52.3, solidly in expansion territory, as an improvement
in services activity offset slower output growth in manufacturing. The
Manufacturing PMI disappointed at 53.3 (-0.8 m/m; an 8-month low).
Underlying price pressures, meanwhile, extended. Headline CPI was
